Adham, ‘Abd Allāh ibn al-Mubārak, Hudhayfah al-Mar‘ashī, Yūsuf ibn Asbāt, and others: they guarded the Syrian frontiers. Some would come from Khurasan, Iraq, and elsewhere to guard these frontiers, because the people of Syria were the ones fighting the Christians, who are People of the Book.

The Sunan collections report that the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said:

“Whoever is killed by the People of the Book receives the reward of two martyrs.”

This is because these people fight on religious grounds. The unbelieving Turks and others like them, however, do not fight on religious grounds. When the former prevail, they corrupt both religion and political rule. The Turks, by contrast, corrupt political rule and those aspects of religion bound up with it; they do not fight on religious grounds.

For this reason, “Tarsus” is frequently mentioned in works of religious learning and jurisprudence written at that time, because it was a Muslim frontier outpost. Indeed, Ahmad ibn Hanbal, al-Sarī al-Saqatī, and other scholars and religious elders would travel there to guard the frontier. Al-Ma’mūn died nearby.

Thus, most statements by the early scholars concerning the merits of Ascalon, Alexandria, Acre, Qazwīn, and other such places, together with accounts of righteous people who lived there and similar reports, relate to their having been frontier outposts, not to any special quality inherent in those places.

Whether a place is a Muslim frontier outpost or not is an incidental rather than an inherent attribute, just as it may be an abode of Islam, an abode of unbelief, an abode of war, an abode of peace, an abode of knowledge and faith, or an abode of

---

## Page 13138

ignorance and hypocrisy. This varies according to its inhabitants and their characteristics. The three mosques, by contrast, possess an enduring distinction of which they cannot be divested. As for other mosques, the scholars disagree over whether they may be altered for a beneficial purpose and put to another use, as ‘Umar ibn al-Khattāb—may Allah be pleased with him—did with the mosque of Kufa: he relocated the mosque, and its former site became shops for date sellers. This is the position of Imam Ahmad and others.

Section

With this understood, these Syrian coastal regions remained Muslim frontier outposts until sometime in the fourth century AH. The Muslims had conquered “Cyprus” during the caliphate of ‘Uthmān—may Allah be pleased with him—with Mu‘āwiyah leading its conquest. During the fourth century, however, the affairs of the caliphate fell into turmoil, and the Rāfidah, the hypocrites, and others acquired power and dominion in Egypt, the Maghrib, the eastern lands, and Syria. These people gained control over various parts of Syria, both coastal and inland. They are a forsaken people, possessing neither reason nor revealed evidence, neither sound religion nor success in worldly affairs.

The Christians consequently overran most of the Syrian coast—indeed, most of Syria itself. They subdued the Rāfidah, the hypocrites, and others, and seized from them what they seized, until the way was made easy by

---

## Page 13139

Allah Most High, through the rule of Sunni kings such as Nūr al-Dīn, Saladin, and others, who rescued most of Syria from the Christians. Remnants of the Rāfidah and the hypocrites remained in Mount Lebanon and elsewhere. At times, the Christians would wrest control of it from them, until these Rāfidah and hypocrites became farmers working for the Christians. Mount Lebanon and similar places thus passed back and forth between the Christians and the Rāfidah. It possesses no special virtue. Residing among Christians or Rāfidah who prevent a Muslim from openly practising his religion is not prescribed; indeed, it is not even permissible.

Yet some groups who favour withdrawing from people out of asceticism and devotion came to suppose that the merit of this mountain and similar places lay in the seclusion they offered and the permissible fruits growing there that one could eat. They therefore sought it out for that purpose, mistakenly and in error. For Muslims are not prescribed to dwell in mountains, caves, and wildernesses, except when turmoil in the towns forces a person to abandon his religious practice—performing his obligations and refraining from prohibited acts. In that case, a Muslim migrates from a land where he cannot practise his religion to one where he can, for the true emigrant is one who abandons what Allah has forbidden.

At times, some of these devout ascetics may be either wronging themselves or following a moderate course, yet making mistakes for which they are forgiven. As for the foremost—

---

## Page 13140

those brought near to Allah—are those who draw near to Allah Most High through voluntary acts of worship after fulfilling their obligatory duties, as the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said in the authentic hadith he related from Allah Most High:

“My servant draws near to Me through nothing comparable to fulfilling what I have made obligatory upon him. My servant continues to draw near to Me through voluntary acts of worship until I love him. When I love him, I become his hearing with which he hears, his sight with which he sees, his hand with which he grasps, and his foot with which he walks. Through Me he hears, through Me he sees, through Me he grasps, and through Me he walks. If he asks Me, I will surely give him; if he seeks My protection, I will surely protect him. I have never hesitated over anything I do as I hesitate over taking the soul of My believing servant: he dislikes death, and I dislike causing him distress, yet he must undergo it.”

There is no disagreement among Muslims that, as a group, devout ascetics living in towns are superior to those living in deserts and mountains, just as a settled villager is superior to a nomad, and an emigrant to a Bedouin. Allah Most High says:

“The Bedouins are stronger in disbelief and hypocrisy, and more likely not to know the limits of what Allah has revealed to His Messenger.”

And the hadith states:

“Among the major sins is for a man to return to Bedouin life after emigrating.”

This concerns someone living in the desert among a community. What, then, of someone who lives permanently alone on a mountain or in a desert? Such a person misses out on religious benefits just as he misses out on worldly benefits, or nearly so. For Allah’s Hand is over the community, and Satan is with the person who is alone, but farther from two.

---

## Page 13141

Section

As for the belief of some ignorant people that the “forty abdāl” are there, this is ignorance and misguidance. The forty abdāl have never gathered there; nor is such a gathering prescribed for them, nor does it serve any benefit. This belief among the ignorant masses resembles the belief of the Rāfidīs concerning the caliph they regard as “the Proof” and “Master of the Age,” whom they describe as hidden from sight yet present in the towns. They exalt his rank and hope for his blessing, although he does not exist and has no reality.

Whoever makes his religion depend on unknown things and turns away from the guidance and religion of truth with which Allah sent His Prophet is among the misguided, outside the law of Islam. Indeed, in these later times, misguided people have been there—Christians, Nusayrīs and Rāfidīs whom the Muslims fought.

Likewise, many ignorant people and purveyors of falsehood and absurdity claim that “men of the unseen” are there or elsewhere. Their veneration of these men is a form of misguidance through which they have gained sway over ignorant people among the Turks, Bedouins, peasants and general public. Through this they have led them away from the true nature of religion and wrongfully consumed their wealth, as Allah Most High says:

“Indeed, many of the rabbis and monks devour people’s wealth unjustly

---

## Page 13142

and turn people away from the path of Allah.” None of Allah’s prophets or close servants had a body hidden from people’s sight. Yet the true condition of the hearts of many of them—their closeness to Allah, profound knowledge and faith, and pure spiritual states—may remain hidden from people. Thus, in towns, in mosques and among people at large, there may be close servants of Allah whose true state most people do not know. As the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said:

“Many a dishevelled, dust-covered man, dressed in two worn garments and turned away from doors, would have his oath fulfilled by Allah if he swore by Him.”

That is, among those whom people regard with aversion because of their shabby appearance, there may be someone whose oath Allah fulfils. This appearance, however, is not a necessary characteristic. Rather, closeness to Allah is as we have explained in connection with His words:

“Indeed, the close servants of Allah will have no fear, nor will they grieve—those who believed and were mindful of Allah.”

Allah’s close servants are therefore the believers who are mindful of Him, in every lawful walk of life.

Likewise, the account of the man whose entire body grew hair like a goat’s is false and impossible. Admittedly, some misguided ascetics neglect the Sunnah until hair grows thickly over their bodies. Such a person should be instructed to do what the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, was commanded to do: trim the moustache closely, pluck the armpit hair, shave the pubic hair, and the like. If he believes that guidance other than that of the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, is more perfect than his guidance

---

## Page 13143

or that some of Allah’s close servants are free to depart from the Law of Muhammad, peace and blessings be upon him—just as al-Khidr was free to depart from the Law of Moses, peace be upon him—then he is an unbeliever who must be put to death after being called upon to repent. For Moses’ mission was not universal, and al-Khidr was not obliged to follow Moses, peace be upon them both. Rather, al-Khidr said to Moses:

“I possess knowledge from Allah which He has taught me and which you do not know, while you possess knowledge from Allah which He has taught you and which I do not know.”

Muhammad ibn Abd Allāh ibn Abd al-Muttalib, however, is the Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings be upon him, to all jinn and humankind: Arabs and non-Arabs, those near and far, rulers and subjects, ascetics and others. Allah Most High said:

“We have sent you only as a bearer of good tidings and a warner to all mankind.”

And He Most High said:

“Say: ‘O mankind, I am the Messenger of Allah to you all, to whom belongs the dominion of the heavens and the earth.’”

The Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said:

“A prophet used to be sent specifically to his own people, whereas I have been sent to all mankind.”

He is the last of the messengers: no prophet is to be awaited after him, nor any further scripture expected. Rather, he is the last of the prophets, and the Book revealed to him confirms the Scripture that preceded it and stands as a guardian over it. Whoever believes that anyone in all creation—whether a scholar, a worshipper or a king—is free to depart from following and obeying him and accepting the Book and wisdom with which he was sent is an unbeliever. A distinction must be made between the Islamic acts of worship, grounded in faith, prophetic guidance and revealed law, which

---

## Page 13144

Allah, His Messenger and His believing servants love, and the innovated, misguided acts of worship rooted in ignorance, concerning which Allah said:

“Or do they have partners who have ordained for them a religion which Allah has not authorized?”

This distinction holds even if some of the greatest devotees and ascetics have fallen into such practices. In the authentic hadith collections, Anas, may Allah be pleased with him, reported:

“The Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, learned that some of his Companions had made certain statements. One said, ‘As for me, I shall fast without ever taking a day off.’ Another said, ‘As for me, I shall pray all night and never sleep.’ Another said, ‘As for me, I shall not marry women.’ Another said, ‘As for me, I shall not eat meat.’ The Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said, ‘But I fast on some days and not on others; I pray at night and also sleep; I marry women and eat meat. Whoever turns away from my Sunnah is not of me.’”

To turn away from something means neither loving nor desiring it. Rather, such a person loves and desires what opposes the prescribed way that Allah and His Messenger love, and the Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings be upon him, has disowned him. Examples include someone who always goes naked, remains perpetually silent, lives alone in the wilderness permanently, abstains entirely from bread and meat, or embraces lifelong monasticism—doing so as an act of worship and imagining that Allah and His Messenger love this rather than its opposite: dressing appropriately, speaking appropriately, eating appropriately, and the like.

Once this is understood, all the practices mentioned—bowing to the mountain in question or others like it, or to anyone there, or visiting it without intending jihad or some prescribed undertaking—are acts of ignorance and misguidance. Likewise, seeking blessings from the fruit brought from it is among

---

## Page 13145

ignorant innovations resembling Christian and polytheistic practices of misguidance. The well-known hadith relates that Basrah ibn Abī Basrah al-Ghifārī saw Abū Hurayrah, may Allah be pleased with him, after he had travelled to al-Tūr—the mountain on which Allah spoke to Moses. He said:

“Had I seen you before you went there, I would not have let you go, for the Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings be upon him, said: ‘Journeys should not be undertaken except to three mosques: al-Masjid al-Harām, al-Masjid al-Aqsā, and this mosque of mine.’”

If travelling to visit al-Tūr—where Allah spoke to Moses and which He called “the sacred valley” and “the blessed spot”—is not prescribed, then what of travelling to visit other mountains? For tūr means “mountain,” and atwār means “mountains.”

As for the well-known grave at its foot in al-Karak, said to be “Noah’s grave,” this claim is utterly false and untenable. No one possessing knowledge and understanding has said that this is Noah’s grave, or the grave of any prophet or righteous person. Indeed, there was never any mention or report of this grave at all. Rather, until quite recently, the place was a plot of land where crops were grown and weavers worked.

People saw a grave there containing a large bone and noticed a smell coming from it. The ignorant supposed that this smell meant it must be a prophet’s grave. They asked, “Which of the prophets was large in stature?” They answered, “Noah.” So they declared, “It is Noah’s grave.” They built the tomb over it during the rule of the Rāfidah (Rejectionists) who were allied with al-Nāsir, ruler of Aleppo. It was later enlarged during the reign of al-Zāhir, until it became an idol through which the ignorant associate partners with Allah.

---

## Page 13146

And it is authentically reported that the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said:

“Allah has forbidden the earth to consume the flesh of the prophets.”

Thus, had this been a prophet’s grave, the bone would not have been stripped of its flesh. Trustworthy local people have related to me, on the authority of their fathers, that they saw those large bones there and had previously seen the site being used for cultivation and weaving. Trustworthy people have also told me that they saw enormous skulls in nearby burial grounds, proportionate to those bones. This establishes that these and similar remains are the bones of the Amalekites who lived in ancient times, or of others like them.

Even if it were the grave of a prophet or a righteous man, building a mosque over it would not be prescribed, according to the consensus of the Muslims and the widely transmitted Sunnah of the Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings be upon him. As recorded in the authentic hadith collections, he said:

“May Allah curse the Jews and the Christians: they took the graves of their prophets as places of worship.”

He also said:

“Those before you used to take graves as places of worship. So do not take graves as places of worship, for I forbid you to do so.”

By the consensus of the Muslims, prayer at the grave of a prophet or anyone else is not recommended, whether obligatory or voluntary. Rather, it is prohibited, and many scholars hold that it is invalid because of the scriptural texts concerning this matter. The places Allah loves, and in which He loves prayer and worship to be offered, are the mosques, concerning which He said:

“In houses which Allah has ordered to be raised and His name to be remembered therein, He is glorified morning and evening.”

He, the Exalted, also said:

“The mosques of Allah are maintained only by those who believe in Allah and the Last Day, establish prayer, and give

---

## Page 13147

zakah, and fear none but Allah. It is they who may be rightly guided.”

The Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, was asked, “Which places are most beloved to Allah?” He replied, “The mosques.” He was then asked, “Which places are most hateful to Allah?” He replied, “The marketplaces.”

He, peace and blessings be upon him, also said:

“Whoever goes to the mosque in the morning or evening, Allah prepares hospitality for him each time he goes.”

He also said:

“When a servant purifies himself, performing ablution thoroughly, then leaves for the mosque solely to pray, one of his footsteps raises him in rank and the other erases a sin.”

The religion of Islam consists in following the various things beloved to Allah with which He sent His Messenger, and avoiding what Allah and His Messenger dislike: religious innovations, deviations, and all kinds of prohibited things.

Islamic acts of worship include the prescribed prayers, congregational prayers, Friday prayers, recitation of the Quran, the remembrance of Allah that He has prescribed for His believing servants, supplication to Him, and the accompanying states of the heart and bodily deeds. They also include the various forms of zakah, charitable giving, and all other kindness to creation, for every good deed is charity. The same applies to all other prescribed acts of worship.

We ask Allah the Most Great to keep us and all our fellow believers steadfast in these acts. And Allah, glorified is He, knows best.

---

## Page 13148

Ahmad ibn Taymiyyah—may Allah Most High have mercy on him—was asked:

What of someone who visits graves and appeals to the person buried there for help with an illness affecting himself, his horse, or his camel, asking for the illness to be removed? He says, “My master, I am under your protection; I am in your keeping. So-and-so has wronged me; so-and-so intends to harm me.” He says that the person buried there is an intermediary between him and Allah Most High.

What of someone who vows to give dirhams, camels, sheep, candles, oil, and other things to mosques, lodges, and shaykhs—living or dead—saying, “If my child comes through safely, I shall owe the shaykh such-and-such,” and similar statements?

What of someone who seeks relief from his shaykh, asking him to steady his heart in the face of what has befallen him? What of someone who comes to his shaykh, touches the grave, rubs his face against it, strokes it with his hands, and then wipes his face with them, and does similar things?

What of someone who approaches him with a need and says, “O so-and-so, by your blessing,” or says, “My need was fulfilled through the blessing of Allah and the blessing of the shaykh”?

What of someone who holds a devotional listening session, comes to the grave, uncovers himself, and places his face on the ground before his shaykh in prostration?

What of someone who says that there exists an all-encompassing spiritual pole and source of succour? Give us your ruling—may you be rewarded—and explain the matter fully.

He replied:

All praise is due to Allah, Lord of all worlds. The religion with which Allah sent His messengers

---

## Page 13149

and revealed His Books is to worship Allah alone, without partner, to seek His help, to rely upon Him, and to call upon Him to obtain benefits and ward off harm. As the Most High said:

“The revelation of this Book is from Allah, the Almighty, the All-Wise.”

“Indeed, We have sent down the Book to you in truth, so worship Allah, devoting your religion sincerely to Him.”

“Indeed, sincere devotion belongs to Allah alone. Those who take protectors besides Him say, ‘We worship them only so that they may bring us nearer to Allah.’ Surely Allah will judge between them concerning that over which they differ.”

The Most High also said:

“The places of worship belong to Allah, so do not invoke anyone alongside Allah.”

The Most High also said:

“Say, ‘My Lord has commanded justice. Turn your faces toward Him at every place of worship, and call upon Him, devoting your religion sincerely to Him.’”

The Most High also said:

“Say, ‘Call upon those whom you claim besides Him. They have no power to remove harm from you or to divert it.’”

“Those whom they invoke themselves seek a means of drawing near to their Lord, each striving to be nearest. They hope for His mercy and fear His punishment. Indeed, your Lord’s punishment is ever to be feared.”

Some of the early Muslims said that certain people used to invoke the Messiah, Ezra, and the angels. Allah Most High said: “Those whom you invoke are My servants, just as you are My servants. They hope for My mercy, just as you hope for My mercy; they fear My punishment, just as you fear My punishment; and they seek to draw near to Me, just as you seek to draw near to Me.” If this is the situation of those who invoke the prophets and angels, what, then, of those who invoke others below them?

The Most High also said:

“Do those who disbelieve think that they can take My servants as protectors instead of Me? Indeed, We have prepared Hell as a lodging for the disbelievers.”

The Most High also said: “Say, ‘Call upon

---

## Page 13150

those whom you claim besides Allah. They possess not even an atom’s weight in the heavens or on earth, nor do they have any share in either, nor does He have any helper among them.’”

“Intercession does not benefit with Him except for one to whom He has given permission.”

Thus, He, glorified be He, has made clear that all created beings invoked besides Allah—angels, humans, and others—possess not even an atom’s weight in His dominion, and that He has no partner in His dominion. Rather, to Him alone belongs all dominion and all praise, and He has power over all things. He has no helper to assist Him, as a king has helpers and supporters, and those who intercede with Him intercede only for those with whom He is pleased. He thereby ruled out the grounds for associating partners with Him.

This is because whoever they invoke besides Him either possesses dominion or does not. If he does not possess dominion, he is either a partner or not a partner. If he is not a partner, he is either a helper or a petitioner making a request. The first three possibilities—possession of dominion, partnership, and assistance—are ruled out. As for the fourth, it can occur only after His permission, as the Most High said:

“Who is it that can intercede with Him except by His permission?”

The Most High also said:

“How many angels there are in the heavens whose intercession avails nothing, except after Allah has given permission for whom He wills and is pleased with.”

The Most High also said:

“Or have they taken intercessors besides Allah? Say, ‘Even though they possess nothing and have no understanding?’”

“Say, ‘All intercession belongs to Allah. To Him belongs the dominion of the heavens and the earth.’”

The Most High also said: “Allah is the One who created the heavens

---

## Page 13151

and the earth and all that is between them in six days, then rose over the Throne. You have no protector or intercessor besides Him. Will you not then take heed?”

The Most High also said:

“And warn with it those who fear being gathered before their Lord, having no protector or intercessor besides Him, so that they may become mindful of Him.”

The Most High also said:

“It is not for a human being whom Allah has given the Scripture, wisdom, and prophethood to then say to people, ‘Be servants of mine instead of Allah.’ Rather, ‘Be godly scholars because of your teaching of the Scripture and your study of it.’”

“Nor would he command you to take the angels and prophets as lords. Would he command you to disbelieve after you have become Muslims?”

If someone who takes the angels and prophets as lords is deemed an unbeliever, what then of someone who takes as lords those beneath them, such as shaykhs and others?

To explain in detail: if what a servant seeks is something that none but Allah Most High can bring about—such as healing for a sick person or animal in his care; repayment of his debt from no specified source; well-being for his family and relief from the afflictions he suffers in this world and the Hereafter; victory over his enemy; guidance for his heart; forgiveness of his sins; admission to Paradise; deliverance from the Fire; learning knowledge and the Quran; or setting his heart right, improving his character, and purifying his soul, and similar things—then all these things may be sought only from Allah Most High. It is not permissible to say to an angel, a prophet, or a shaykh—whether alive or dead—“Forgive my sin,” “Grant me victory over my enemy,” “Heal my sick one,” or “Restore my health, or that of my family or my riding animal,”

---

## Page 13152

or anything similar.

Whoever asks any created being for such things, whoever that being may be, associates partners with his Lord and is of the same kind as the polytheists who worship angels, prophets, and statues fashioned in their likenesses. This is also akin to the Christians’ invocation of the Messiah and his mother. Allah Most High said:

“And when Allah says, ‘O Jesus, son of Mary, did you tell people, “Take me and my mother as two gods besides Allah”?’”

And the verse continues. The Most High also said:

“They have taken their rabbis and monks as lords besides Allah, and the Messiah, son of Mary, though they were commanded to worship only One God. There is no god but Him. Glory be to Him, far above the partners they associate with Him!”

As for what a person is capable of doing, it is permissible to ask him for it in some circumstances but not others. Thus, “making requests of created beings” may be permissible or may be forbidden. Allah Most High said:

“So when you have finished, strive in worship.”

“And turn your longing to your Lord.”

The Prophet, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace, advised Ibn ‘Abbās:

“When you ask, ask Allah; and when you seek help, seek help from Allah.”

The Prophet, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace, also instructed a group of his Companions not to ask people for anything. Thus, if one of them dropped his whip, he would not say to anyone, “Hand it to me.” It is authentically established in the two Sahīh collections that he, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace, said:

“Seventy thousand of my nation will enter Paradise without being called to account. They are those who do not ask others to perform ruqyah for them, do not seek cauterization, do not take omens, and place their trust in their Lord.”

Asking for ruqyah means requesting a healing recitation, which is a form of supplication. Nevertheless, it is also authentically reported that he, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace, said: “There is no

---

## Page 13153

man for whom his brother supplicates in his absence but Allah appoints an angel for that supplication. Whenever he supplicates for his brother, the angel says: ‘And may you have the same.’”

Among the prescribed forms of supplication is praying for another in his absence. Thus, the Prophet, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace, instructed us to invoke blessings upon him and ask Allah to grant him al-wasīlah. He also told us of the reward we receive for making these supplications, saying in the hadith:

“When you hear the caller to prayer, repeat what he says, then invoke blessings upon me, for whoever invokes blessings upon me once, Allah will bless him ten times. Then ask Allah to grant me al-wasīlah, for it is a station in Paradise befitting only one of Allah’s servants, and I hope to be that servant. Whoever asks Allah to grant me al-wasīlah will be entitled to my intercession on the Day of Resurrection.”

It is prescribed for a Muslim to request supplication from someone of either higher or lower standing. Requests for supplication from both those above and those below have been reported. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace, bade farewell to ‘Umar as he set out for ‘umrah and said:

“Do not forget us in your supplications, my brother.”

However, when he, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace, instructed us to invoke blessings upon him and ask Allah to grant him al-wasīlah, he explained that whoever invokes blessings upon him once receives ten blessings from Allah in return, and that whoever asks for al-wasīlah for him will be entitled to his intercession on the Day of Resurrection. Thus, his request of us was for our benefit. There is a difference between someone who requests something from another for that person’s benefit and someone who asks another solely because he needs him.

It is also established in the Sahīh that he, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace, mentioned Uways al-Qaranī and said to ‘Umar:

“If you can have him ask forgiveness for you, then do so.”

---

## Page 13154

It is also related in the two Sahīh collections that a disagreement arose between Abū Bakr and ‘Umar, may Allah be pleased with them both, and Abū Bakr said to ‘Umar, “Ask forgiveness for me.” However, the hadith mentions that Abū Bakr acknowledged having become angry with ‘Umar. It is also established that some people asked to have healing prayers recited over them, and the Prophet, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace, would recite over them.

It is established in the two Sahīh collections that:

“When the people suffered drought, they asked the Prophet, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace, to pray for rain for them. He supplicated to Allah for them, and they were given rain.”

It is also related in the two Sahīh collections that ‘Umar ibn al-Khattāb, may Allah be pleased with him, sought rain through al-‘Abbās, supplicating:

“O Allah, when we suffered drought, we would seek to draw near to You through our Prophet, and You would grant us rain. Now we seek to draw near to You through our Prophet’s uncle, so grant us rain.”

They would then be given rain.

It is related in the Sunan collections that a Bedouin said to the Prophet, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace:

“People are suffering hardship, dependants are starving, and wealth has been lost. So supplicate to Allah for us, for we seek Allah’s intercession with you, and your intercession with Allah.”

The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace, then glorified Allah until this was reflected in his Companions’ faces. He said:

“Woe to you! Allah is not sought as an intercessor with any of His creation. Allah’s majesty is far greater than that.”

Thus, he approved his statement, “We seek your intercession with Allah,” but rejected his statement, “We seek Allah’s intercession with you.” This is because an intercessor makes a request of the one whose favour is sought. The servant asks his Lord and seeks intercession with Him, whereas the Lord, Most High, neither asks the servant nor seeks his intercession.

As for “prescribed grave visitation,” it consists of greeting the deceased and supplicating for him, much like offering the funeral prayer for him, just as the Prophet, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace, used to

---

## Page 13155

teach his Companions, when they visited graves, to say:

“Peace be upon you, inhabitants of this abode of believers. We will, if Allah wills, join you. May Allah have mercy on those among us and you who have gone before and those who are to follow. We ask Allah for well-being for ourselves and for you. O Allah, do not deprive us of reward on their account, nor put us to trial after them.”

It is related that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace, said:

“No man passes by the grave of someone he knew in this world and greets him without Allah restoring the deceased’s soul to him so that he may return the greeting.”

Allah Most High rewards the living person when he supplicates for a deceased believer, just as He rewards him when he offers the funeral prayer for him. For this reason, the Prophet, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace, forbade doing this for the hypocrites. Allah, exalted is He, said:

“Never pray over any of them who dies, nor stand at his grave.”

Thus, prescribed grave visitation does not involve the living needing the deceased, asking anything of him, or seeking to draw near to Allah through him. Rather, it involves the living benefiting the deceased, as when offering the funeral prayer for him. Allah Most High shows mercy to the deceased through the living person’s supplication and kindness toward him, and rewards the living person for his deed. For it is established in the Sahīh that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace, said:

“When a son of Adam dies, his deeds come to an end except for three: ongoing charity, knowledge from which people benefit after his death, or a righteous child who supplicates for him.”

---

## Page 13156

Section

As for someone who goes to the grave of a prophet or a righteous person—or to what he believes to be such a grave, though it is not—and petitions him and seeks his aid, this falls into three degrees:

One is to ask him to fulfil a need, such as curing his illness or that of his animals, settling his debt, taking vengeance on his enemy for him, or granting well-being to him, his family, and his animals, and other such things that none but Allah, Mighty and Majestic, can do. This is explicit polytheism. The person who does this must be called upon to repent, and if he does not repent, he is to be put to death.

Even if he says, “I ask him because he is closer to Allah than I am, so that he may intercede for me in these matters; for I seek access to Allah through him, just as one seeks access to a ruler through his intimates and aides,” this is among the practices of the polytheists and Christians. They claim that they take their religious scholars and monks as intercessors, seeking their intercession to obtain what they want. Likewise, Allah tells us that the polytheists said:

“We worship them only so that they may bring us nearer to Allah.”

And He, glorified and exalted is He, said:

“Or have they taken intercessors besides Allah? Say, ‘Even though they possess nothing and have no understanding?’”

“Say, ‘All intercession belongs to Allah. To Him belongs the dominion of the heavens

---

## Page 13157

and the earth. Then to Him you will be returned.’”

And He, exalted is He, said:

“You have no protector or intercessor besides Him. Will you not then take heed?”

And He, exalted is He, said:

“Who is it that can intercede with Him except by His permission?”

Thus He made clear the distinction between Himself and His creation.

People customarily seek the intercession of someone held in esteem by one of their leaders. That intercessor then makes a request of the leader, who fulfils the petitioner’s need out of desire, fear, embarrassment, affection, or some other motive. But no one intercedes with Allah, glorified is He, until He Himself grants the intercessor permission. He does only what He wills, and the intercessor’s intercession itself depends on His permission. Thus the entire matter belongs to Him.

For this reason, the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him and his family, said in the hadith narrated by Abū Hurayrah, may Allah be pleased with him, and recorded by both al-Bukhārī and Muslim:

“None of you should say, ‘O Allah, forgive me if You will; O Allah, have mercy on me if You will.’ Rather, let him be resolute in his request, for no one can compel Allah.”

He thus explained that the Lord, glorified is He, does whatever He wills. No one compels Him in what He chooses to do, whereas an intercessor may compel the person with whom he intercedes, and a petitioner may compel the person he petitions by persisting and troubling him with his requests.

Longing must therefore be directed to Him, as He, exalted is He, said:

“So when you have finished, strive hard, and direct your longing to your Lord.”

And fear must be of Allah, as He, exalted is He, said:

“And fear Me alone.”

And He, exalted is He, said:

“So do not fear people, but fear Me.”

He has commanded us to invoke blessings upon the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him and his family, when supplicating, and has made this one of the means by which our supplications are answered.

---

## Page 13158

The claim made by many misguided people, “This person is closer to Allah than I am, while I am far from Allah; I cannot supplicate to Him except through this intermediary,” and similar claims are among the sayings of the polytheists. For Allah, exalted is He, says:

“When My servants ask you about Me, I am indeed near. I answer the supplication of the supplicant when he calls upon Me.”

It has been reported that the Companions said, “O Messenger of Allah, is our Lord near, so that we may address Him quietly, or distant, so that we must call out to Him?” Allah then revealed this verse.

It is related in the Sahīh that they were on a journey and were raising their voices in proclaiming Allah’s greatness. The Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him and his family, said:

“O people, be easy on yourselves. You are not calling upon one who is deaf or absent; rather, you are calling upon One who hears and is near. Indeed, the One upon whom you call is nearer to each of you than the neck of his mount.”

Allah, exalted is He, has commanded all His servants to pray to Him and address Him privately, and has commanded each of them to say:

“You alone we worship, and You alone we ask for help.”

He has also told us that the polytheists said:

“We worship them only so that they may bring us nearer to Allah.”

Then this polytheist is asked: When you call upon this person, if you think that he knows your condition better, is more capable of granting your request, or is more merciful towards you, then this is ignorance, misguidance, and disbelief. But if you know that Allah is more knowledgeable, more capable, and more merciful, why have you turned away from asking Him to ask someone else?

Will you not heed what al-Bukhārī and others narrated from Jābir, may Allah be pleased with him, who said:

“The Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings be upon him and his family, used to teach us how to seek Allah’s guidance in our affairs just as he would teach us a surah of the Quran. He would say, ‘When any of you is considering a matter

---

## Page 13159

, let him offer two units of prayer other than the obligatory prayer, then say:

“O Allah, I seek Your guidance through Your knowledge, and I seek ability through Your power, and I ask You of Your immense bounty. For You have power and I have none; You know and I do not, and You are the Knower of all unseen things. O Allah, if You know that this matter is good for me in my religion, my livelihood, and the outcome of my affairs, then decree it for me, make it easy for me, and bless it for me. But if You know that this matter is bad for me in my religion, my livelihood, and the outcome of my affairs, then turn it away from me and turn me away from it. Decree what is good for me wherever it may be, then make me content with it.” He said, “And he should specify his need.”’”

He instructed the servant to say: “I seek Your guidance through Your knowledge, and I seek ability through Your power, and I ask You of Your immense bounty.”

If you know that this person is closer to Allah than you are and holds a higher rank with Allah, that is true—but it is a true statement used to advance falsehood. For his being closer and higher in rank than you means only that Allah rewards him and gives him more than He gives you. It does not mean that, if you call upon him, Allah will fulfil your need more fully than if you called upon Allah, exalted is He, yourself.

If you deserve punishment and the rejection of your supplication—for example, because it contains transgression—then neither a prophet nor a righteous person will assist in what Allah dislikes or strive for what Allah hates. If that is not the case, then Allah is more worthy of showing mercy and accepting your supplication.

If you say, “When this person calls upon Allah, Allah answers his supplication more fully than He answers mine when I call upon Him,” then this is the “second category”: namely, that you do not ask him to perform the act, nor

---

## Page 13160

call upon him, but ask him to supplicate for you, just as you say to a living person, “Supplicate for me,” and as the Companions—may Allah be pleased with them—used to ask the Prophet, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace, to supplicate. This is prescribed with regard to the living, as explained earlier.

As for the dead, whether prophets, righteous people, or others, it has not been prescribed for us to say to them, “Supplicate for us,” or, “Ask your Lord on our behalf.” None of the Companions or Successors did this, none of the imams instructed people to do it, and no hadith has been reported prescribing it. Rather, the established report in the Sahīh states that when they suffered drought during the time of ‘Umar—may Allah be pleased with him—he sought rain through al-‘Abbās and said: “O Allah, when we suffered drought, we used to seek to draw near to You through our Prophet, and You would grant us rain. Now we seek to draw near to You through our Prophet’s uncle, so grant us rain.” They would then be granted rain.

They did not come to the grave of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace, saying, “O Messenger of Allah, supplicate to Allah for us and ask Him to grant us rain. We complain to you of what has befallen us,” or anything similar. Not a single Companion ever did this. Rather, it is an innovation for which Allah has sent down no authority. When they came to the grave of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace, they would greet him with peace. If they wished to supplicate, they would not supplicate to Allah facing the noble grave. Instead, they would turn away, face the qiblah, and supplicate to Allah alone, without any partner, just as they supplicated to Him everywhere else.

For it is reported in al-Muwatta’ and elsewhere that he, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace, said: “O Allah, do not make my grave an idol that is worshipped. Allah’s wrath was severe against people who took

---

## Page 13161

the graves of their prophets as places of worship.”

It is reported in the Sunan that he said: “Do not make my grave a place of festivity, and send blessings upon me wherever you are, for your blessings reach me.”

It is reported in the Sahīh that, during the illness from which he did not recover, he said: “May Allah curse the Jews and Christians; they took the graves of their prophets as places of worship.” He was warning against what they had done. ‘Ā’ishah—may Allah be pleased with her and her parents—said: “Were it not for that, his grave would have been left exposed, but it was disliked that it should be made a place of worship.”

In Sahīh Muslim, it is reported that he, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace, said five days before his death: “Those who came before you used to take graves as places of worship. Beware! Do not take graves as places of worship, for I forbid you to do so.”

In Sunan Abī Dāwūd, it is reported that he said: “May Allah curse women who visit graves and those who establish places of worship and lamps over them.”

For this reason, our scholars said that it is not permissible to build a mosque over graves. They also said that it is not permissible to vow anything for a grave or for those residing beside it—whether money, oil, wax, livestock, or anything else. All such vows are vows to disobey Allah. It is established in the Sahīh that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace, said: “Whoever vows to obey Allah, let him obey Him; and whoever vows to disobey Allah, let him not disobey Him.” The scholars differed as to whether someone who makes such a vow must offer the expiation for a broken oath; they held two views.

For this reason, none of the imams of the early generations said that prayer at graves or at grave shrines is recommended or possesses any special merit, or that

---

## Page 13162

prayer and supplication there are better than prayer and supplication elsewhere. Rather, they all agreed that prayer in mosques and homes is better than prayer at graves—the graves of prophets and righteous people—whether or not they are called “shrines.” Allah and His Messenger have prescribed certain things specifically for mosques, not shrines. Allah Most High says:

“Who is more unjust than those who prevent Allah’s name from being mentioned in His mosques and strive to destroy them?”

He did not say “shrines.” Allah Most High also says:

“While you are in devotional retreat in the mosques.”

He did not say “in shrines.” Allah Most High says:

“Say, ‘My Lord has commanded justice. Direct your faces toward Him at every mosque.’”

Allah Most High says:

“Only those who believe in Allah and the Last Day, establish prayer, give zakāh, and fear none but Allah maintain Allah’s mosques. It is they who may be among the rightly guided.”

Allah Most High says:

“The mosques belong to Allah, so do not invoke anyone alongside Allah.”

The Prophet, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace, said:

“A man’s prayer in the mosque is twenty-five times better than his prayer in his home or marketplace.”

He also said, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace:

“Whoever builds a mosque for Allah, Allah will build him a house in Paradise.”

As for graves, it has been reported that he, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace, forbade taking them as places of worship and cursed those who do so. This was mentioned by a number of the Companions and Successors, as recorded by al-Bukhārī in his Sahīh, by al-Tabarī and others in their Quran commentaries, and by Wathīmah and others in Stories of the Prophets, in connection with the words of Allah Most High: “And they said:

---

## Page 13163

‘Never abandon your gods; never abandon Wadd, Suwāʿ, Yaghūth, Yaʿūq, or Nasr.’” They explained:

These were the names of righteous men among Noah’s people. When they died, people devoted themselves to staying at their graves. Then, after a long time had passed, they made their images into idols.

Devoting oneself to staying at graves, rubbing one’s hands over them, kissing them, supplicating beside and among them, and similar practices were the origin of associating partners with Allah and worshipping idols. For this reason, the Prophet, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace, said:

“O Allah, do not let my grave become an idol that is worshipped.”

The scholars agreed that anyone visiting the grave of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace, or the grave of any other prophet or righteous person—including the Companions, the Prophet’s household, and others—must neither rub his hands over it nor kiss it. Indeed, there is no inanimate object in the world that it is prescribed to kiss except the Black Stone. It is established in the two Sahīh collections that ʿUmar, may Allah be pleased with him, said:

“By Allah, I know that you are a stone that can neither harm nor benefit. Had I not seen the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace, kiss you, I would not have kissed you.”

For this reason, the imams agree that it is not a recommended practice to kiss or touch with one’s hand the two corners of the House adjoining the Hijr enclosure, the walls of the House, the Station of Abraham, the Rock in Jerusalem, or the grave of any prophet or righteous person.

The jurists even differed over placing one’s hand on the pulpit of our master, the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace, when it still existed. Mālik and others disapproved of this because it was a religious innovation. It is reported that when Mālik saw ʿAtāʾ do this, he declined to take knowledge from him, whereas permission was granted by

---

## Page 13164

Ahmad and others, because Ibn ‘Umar, may Allah be pleased with them both, did so. As for rubbing one’s hands over the grave of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace, and kissing it, they all disapproved of this and forbade it. They understood the Prophet’s intention to eradicate the roots of associating partners with Allah, uphold His oneness, and devote worship sincerely to Allah, Lord of the worlds.

This makes clear the distinction between asking the Prophet, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace, or a righteous man for something during his lifetime, and asking him after his death or in his absence. During his lifetime, no one worships him in his presence. For when the prophets—may Allah Most High be pleased with them all—and the righteous are alive, they do not allow anyone to associate them with Allah in their presence. Rather, they forbid people to do so and punish them for it. Thus the Messiah, peace be upon him, said:

“I told them only what You commanded me: ‘Worship Allah, my Lord and your Lord.’ I was a witness over them as long as I remained among them. But when You took me away, You alone watched over them, and You are a Witness over all things.”

A man said to the Prophet, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace, “Whatever Allah and you will.” He replied, “Have you made me an equal to Allah? Whatever Allah alone wills.”

He also said:

“Do not say, ‘Whatever Allah and Muhammad will,’ but say, ‘Whatever Allah wills, then whatever Muhammad wills.’”

When a young girl said:

“Among us is Allah’s Messenger, who knows what tomorrow holds,”

he said, “Leave that; say what you were saying before.”

He also said, “Do not praise me excessively as the Christians excessively praised the son of Mary. I am only a servant, so say, ‘Allah’s servant and His Messenger.’”

When they stood in rows behind him, he said, “Do not exalt me as non-Arabs exalt one another.” And it was said by

---

## Page 13165

Anas: “No one was dearer to them than the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace. Yet when they saw him, they would not stand up for him, knowing his dislike of that.”

When Mu‘ādh prostrated before him, he forbade him and said:

“Prostration is appropriate only for Allah. Were I to command anyone to prostrate before another, I would command a wife to prostrate before her husband, because of the greatness of his rights over her.”

When the heretics who had gone to extremes concerning ‘Alī and believed him to be divine were brought before him, he ordered them to be burned with fire. Such is the way of Allah’s prophets and His righteous servants. Only those who seek supremacy and corruption on earth allow others to go to extremes concerning them and exalt them without right—people such as Pharaoh and misguided religious leaders whose aim is supremacy and corruption on earth.

Going astray concerning the prophets and the righteous, taking them as lords, and associating them with Allah occurs in their absence and after their deaths, just as the Messiah and Ezra were made partners with Allah. This further clarifies the distinction between making requests of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace, or a righteous person during his lifetime and in his presence, and making requests of him after his death and in his absence.

None of the early generations of this community—in the time of the Companions, their successors, or the successors of their successors—deliberately sought to offer prayers and supplications at the graves of the prophets, made requests of them, or sought their aid, whether in their absence or at their graves. The same applies to devotional stays there.

Among the gravest forms of associating partners with Allah is for a man to seek aid from someone dead or absent, as mentioned by

---

## Page 13166

the questioner—seeking his aid in times of calamity and saying, “O my master, so-and-so!” as though asking him to remove his affliction or bring him some benefit. This is how Christians treat the Messiah, his mother, their religious scholars, and their monks. It is well known that the best of creation and the most honoured before Allah is our Prophet Muhammad, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace. Those who knew his standing and his rights best were his Companions, yet they did none of this, either in his absence or after his death.

These people who associate partners with Allah add lying to their polytheism, for lying goes hand in hand with polytheism. Allah Most High says:

“So shun the impurity of idols and shun false speech, devoting yourselves to Allah alone, associating nothing with Him.”

The Prophet, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace, said:

“False testimony is equivalent to associating partners with Allah.” He repeated this twice or three times.

Allah Most High says:

“Those who took the calf for worship will incur anger from their Lord and humiliation in the life of this world. Thus do We recompense those who fabricate lies.”

Abraham, Allah’s close friend, peace be upon him, said:

“Is it falsehood—gods other than Allah—that you desire? What, then, do you think of the Lord of all worlds?”

Among their lies is the claim one of them makes about his shaykh: if the disciple is in the west and his shaykh is in the east, and the disciple’s covering slips off, the shaykh puts it back over him. If the shaykh cannot do this, they claim, he is no shaykh.

The devils may lead them astray just as they lead idol-worshippers astray. Such things, involving polytheism and sorcery, used to occur among the Arabs in connection with their idols, and among those who worshipped the stars and their talismans. Likewise, the Tatars, Indians, black Africans, and other groups of polytheists experience the devils’ leading them astray, speaking to them, and similar things.

---

## Page 13167

Thus, many of these people may experience something of this kind, especially when listening to whistling and clapping. Devils may descend upon them, and one of them may experience what a person suffering a seizure experiences: frothing, foaming at the mouth, and hideous screaming. A devil may speak to him in words that neither he nor those present understand, and similar things may happen to these misguided people.

The third category is to say: “O Allah, by so-and-so’s standing before You, or by so-and-so’s blessing, or by so-and-so’s honour before You, do such-and-such for me.” Many people do this, but it has not been reported that any of the Companions, the Successors, or the early generations of this community used to offer such supplications. Nor has any scholarly statement on this matter reached me that I can relate, apart from what I have seen in the fatwas of the jurist Abū Muhammad ibn ʿAbd al-Salām. He ruled that no one may do this except with respect to the Prophet, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace—if the hadith concerning the Prophet, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace, is authentic.

The question on which the ruling was sought concerned the following: al-Nasā’ī, al-Tirmidhī, and others narrated that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace, taught some of his Companions to supplicate, saying:

“O Allah, I ask You and seek access to You through Your Prophet, the Prophet of mercy. O Muhammad, O Messenger of Allah, I seek access through you to my Lord concerning my need, that He may fulfil it for me. O Allah, accept his intercession for me.”

Some have cited this hadith as evidence that it is permissible to seek access to Allah through the Prophet, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace, both during his lifetime and after his death. They said: “Seeking access to Allah in this way does not involve calling upon

---

## Page 13168

created beings or seeking their aid; rather, it is supplication to Allah and seeking His aid. It does, however, involve asking Him by virtue of the Prophet’s standing, as related in Sunan Ibn Mājah from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace, who taught a person setting out for prayer to say:

‘O Allah, I ask You by the right that those who ask You have upon You, and by the right of this walk of mine. I have not set out in insolence or arrogance, to show off or to gain a reputation. I have set out to avoid Your wrath and seek Your pleasure. I ask You to save me from the Fire and forgive my sins, for none forgives sins but You.’”

They argued that, in this hadith, he asked by the right that those who ask Allah have upon Him, and by the right of his walk to prayer. Allah Most High has imposed a right upon Himself. Allah Most High says:

“It was incumbent upon Us to help the believers.”

And similarly:

“It is a promise binding upon your Lord, whose fulfilment may be requested.”

In the two Sahīhs, Muʿādh ibn Jabal narrated that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace, said to him:

“O Muʿādh, do you know what Allah’s right over His servants is?”

He replied, “Allah and His Messenger know best.”

He said, “Allah’s right over His servants is that they worship Him and associate nothing with Him. Do you know what the servants’ right upon Allah is if they do that? Their right upon Him is that He should not punish them.”

More than one hadith contains the words, “It is incumbent upon Allah to do such-and-such,” as in his saying:

“Whoever drinks wine, none of his prayers will be accepted for forty days. If he repents, Allah will accept his repentance. If he returns to drinking it for the third or fourth time, it is incumbent upon Allah to give him the mire of corruption to drink.”

He was asked, “What is the mire of corruption?”

He replied:

---

## Page 13169

“The discharge of the people of the Fire.”

Another group said that this does not establish the permissibility of seeking access to Allah through him after his death or in his absence. Rather, it concerns doing so during his lifetime, in his presence, as related in Sahīh al-Bukhārī: ʿUmar ibn al-Khattāb, may Allah be pleased with him, sought rain through al-ʿAbbās, saying:

“O Allah, whenever we suffered drought, we used to seek access to You through our Prophet, and You would send us rain. Now we seek access to You through our Prophet’s uncle, so send us rain.”

Then they would be granted rain. ʿUmar ibn al-Khattāb, may Allah be pleased with him, thus explained that they used to seek access to Allah through the Prophet during his lifetime and would be granted rain. This meant that they would ask him to supplicate to Allah for them; he would supplicate for them, and they would supplicate alongside him, seeking access to Allah through his intercession and supplication. As related in the Sahīh from Anas ibn Mālik, may Allah be pleased with him:

“A man entered the mosque on Friday through a door beside Dār al-Qadā’, while the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace, was standing and delivering the sermon. He stood facing the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace, and said, ‘O Messenger of Allah, the livestock have perished and the roads have been cut off. Supplicate to Allah for us to withhold the rain from us.’

“He said: The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace, raised his hands and said, ‘O Allah, around us, not upon us. O Allah, upon the hills and hillocks, the valley beds, and the places where trees grow.’

“He said: The rain ceased, and we went out walking in the sunshine.”

Thus, in this hadith, the man said, “Supplicate to Allah for us to withhold the rain from us.”

It is also related in the Sahīh that ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUmar said:

“Indeed, I

---

## Page 13170

recall Abū Tālib’s words about the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace:

‘A fair-faced man, through whose countenance rain is sought from the clouds—
A refuge for orphans, a protector of widows.’”

This was how they sought access to Allah through him when seeking rain and the like. When he died, they sought access to Allah through al-‘Abbās, may Allah be pleased with him, just as they had sought access through him and sought rain.

They did not seek rain through him after his death, in his absence, at his grave, or at anyone else’s grave. Likewise, Mu‘āwiyah ibn Abī Sufyān sought rain through Yazīd ibn al-Aswad al-Jurashī, saying:

“O Allah, we seek intercession with You through the best among us. O Yazīd, raise your hands to Allah.”

He raised his hands and supplicated, and they supplicated, and they were granted rain.

For this reason, the scholars said:

“It is recommended to seek rain through righteous and virtuous people. If they belong to the household of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace, that is better still.”

None of the scholars stated that it is prescribed to seek access to Allah or seek rain through the Prophet or a righteous person after his death or in his absence. Nor did they recommend this when seeking rain, seeking victory, or making any other supplications. Supplication is the essence of worship.

Worship is founded upon the Sunnah and adherence to it, not upon personal desires and religious innovation. Allah is to be worshipped only as He has prescribed, not according to personal desires and innovations. Allah Most High says:

“Or do they have partners who have ordained for them in religion what Allah has not permitted?”

And Allah Most High says:

“Call upon your Lord humbly and in secret. Indeed, He does not love those who transgress.”

And in the words of

---

## Page 13171

the Prophet, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace:

“There will be people in this community who transgress the bounds in supplication and purification.”

As for a man who, when calamity befalls him or he fears something, calls upon his shaykh for help, seeking to steady his heart in the face of what has happened: this is a form of polytheism and is akin to the religion of the Christians. For Allah alone bestows mercy and removes harm. Allah Most High says:

“If Allah touches you with harm, none can remove it except Him; and if He intends good for you, none can repel His bounty.”

Allah Most High also says:

“Whatever mercy Allah opens up for people, none can withhold; and whatever He withholds, none can release thereafter.”

Allah Most High also says:

“Say, ‘Tell me: if Allah’s punishment came upon you, or the Hour came upon you, would you call upon anyone other than Allah, if you are truthful?’

“No! It is Him alone you would call upon, and He would remove the affliction for which you called upon Him, if He willed, and you would forget those you associate with Him.”

Allah Most High also says:

“Say, ‘Call upon those whom you claim to be gods besides Him. They have no power to remove harm from you or divert it.’

“Those whom they invoke themselves seek a means of drawing near to their Lord, each striving to be nearest. They hope for His mercy and fear His punishment. Indeed, your Lord’s punishment is ever to be feared.”

He thus makes clear that the angels, prophets, and others who are invoked have no power to remove harm from those who invoke them or to divert it.

If someone says, “I call upon the shaykh so that he may intercede for me,” this is of the same kind as the Christians’ invocation of Mary, religious scholars, and monks. The believer hopes in his Lord, fears Him, and calls upon Him with sincere devotion to Him alone. What he owes his shaykh is to pray for him and ask Allah to have mercy on him. For the greatest of all created beings

---

## Page 13172

in rank is the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace. His Companions knew his commands and his rank better than anyone and were the most obedient to him. Yet he never instructed any of them, in moments of alarm and fear, to say, “O my master, O Messenger of Allah!” Nor did they do so during his lifetime or after his death. Rather, he instructed them to remember Allah, supplicate to Him, and invoke blessings and peace upon him, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace.

Allah Most High says:

“Those to whom people said, ‘The people have gathered against you, so fear them,’ only increased in faith and said, ‘Allah is sufficient for us, and He is the best Protector.’

“So they returned with blessings and bounty from Allah, untouched by harm. They pursued Allah’s pleasure, and Allah is the Possessor of immense bounty.”

In Sahīh al-Bukhārī, Ibn ʿAbbās, may Allah be pleased with them both, reported that Abraham, peace be upon him, said these words when he was cast into the fire. Muhammad, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace—meaning he and his Companions—also said them when people told them, “The people have gathered against you.”

It is reported in al-Sahīh that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace, would say in times of distress:

“There is no god but Allah, the Magnificent, the Forbearing. There is no god but Allah, Lord of the noble Throne. There is no god but Allah, Lord of the heavens and the earth and Lord of the mighty Throne.”

It has also been reported that he taught a similar supplication to some members of his household. In the Sunan, it is reported that whenever the Prophet, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace, was beset by a difficulty, he would say:

“O Ever-Living, O Sustainer of all, I seek help through Your mercy.”

It is also reported that he taught his daughter Fātimah to say:

“O Ever-Living, O Sustainer of all, O Originator of the heavens and the earth, there is no god but You. I seek help through Your mercy.

---

## Page 13173

Set all my affairs right, and do not leave me to myself even for the blink of an eye, nor to anyone among Your creation.”

In the Musnad of Imam Ahmad and the Sahīh of Abū Hātim al-Bustī, Ibn Masʿūd, may Allah be pleased with him, reported that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace, said:

“Whenever a servant is afflicted by anxiety or sorrow and says, ‘O Allah, I am Your servant, the son of Your male servant and Your female servant. My forelock is in Your hand. Your judgment concerning me takes effect, and Your decree concerning me is just. I ask You by every name belonging to You with which You have named Yourself, which You have revealed in Your Book, which You have taught to any of Your creation, or which You have kept to Yourself in the knowledge of the unseen with You, to make the magnificent Quran the springtime of my heart, the light of my breast, the clearing of my sorrow, and the removal of my anxiety and distress,’ Allah removes his anxiety and distress and replaces them with joy.”

They asked, “O Messenger of Allah, should we not learn these words?” He replied, “Whoever hears them should learn them.”

He also said to his community:

“The sun and the moon are two signs among the signs of Allah. They are not eclipsed because of anyone’s death or birth; rather, through them Allah strikes fear into His servants. When you see that, hasten to prayer, the remembrance of Allah, and seeking forgiveness.”

Thus, at the time of an eclipse, he commanded them to pray, supplicate, remember Allah, free slaves, and give charity. He did not command them to call upon any created being—whether an angel, a prophet, or anyone else.

There are many similar examples in his Sunnah. In times of fear, nothing has been prescribed for Muslims except what Allah has commanded: supplicating to Allah, remembering Him, seeking forgiveness, praying, and giving charity

---

## Page 13174

and the like. How, then, can someone who believes in Allah and His Messenger turn away from what Allah and His Messenger have prescribed to a religious innovation for which Allah has granted no authority, resembling the religion of the polytheists and Christians?

If someone claims that his need was fulfilled through such practices, that his shaykh was made to appear before him, or something similar, those who worship celestial bodies and idols, and other polytheists, experience the same things. This has been abundantly reported of polytheists both past and present. Were it not for this, idols and the like would not have been worshipped. Allah’s intimate friend, peace be upon him, said:

“And keep me and my sons from worshipping idols.”

“My Lord, they have indeed led many people astray.”

It is said that polytheism first appeared in the land of Mecca after Abraham, Allah’s intimate friend, through ʿAmr ibn Luhayy al-Khuzāʿī, whom the Prophet, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace, saw dragging his intestines in the Fire. He was the first to institute the practice of setting consecrated animals loose and to alter the religion of Abraham. They relate that he travelled to Greater Syria and found idols there in al-Balqāʾ. The people claimed that these helped them secure benefits and ward off harm, so he brought them to Mecca and introduced polytheism and idol worship among the Arabs.

As for the things Allah and His Messenger have forbidden—polytheism, sorcery, killing, unlawful sexual intercourse, false testimony, drinking wine, and other prohibited acts—the soul may find in them something it regards as a benefit or as protection from harm. Were it not for that, souls would not venture upon forbidden acts, in which there is no good whatsoever. Souls are led into forbidden acts only by ignorance

---

## Page 13175

or need. As for someone who knows that a thing is evil and forbidden, how could he do it? Those who engage in all these practices may be ignorant of the harm they involve, or they may feel a need for them, such as a craving for them. The harm they entail may outweigh their pleasure, yet people fail to realize this because of their ignorance, or their desires overpower them until they commit these acts. Desire often makes a person behave as though he knows nothing of the truth, for your love of something blinds and deafens you.

This is why the person of knowledge fears Allah. Abū al-Āliyah said: “I asked the Companions of Muhammad, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace, about the verse in which Allah, Mighty and Majestic, says:

‘Allah only accepts the repentance of those who do evil in ignorance and then repent soon afterwards.’

They replied: ‘Everyone who disobeys Allah is ignorant, and everyone who repents before death has repented soon afterwards.’”

This is not the place to explain at length the predominant harms in what is forbidden and the predominant benefits in what is commanded. Rather, it is enough for the believer to know that whatever Allah commands is purely or predominantly beneficial, and whatever He forbids is purely or predominantly harmful. Allah has not commanded His servants to do what He commands because He needs them, nor has He forbidden them what He forbids in order to deceive them. Rather, He commands them to do what brings them well-being and forbids them from what brings them harm. Hence He described His Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, as one who

“enjoins upon them what is right and forbids them what is wrong, makes lawful for them what is wholesome and forbids them what is foul.”

As for rubbing oneself against a grave—whatever grave it may be—kissing it, and rubbing one’s cheek upon it

---

## Page 13176

—these practices are forbidden by the agreement of the Muslims, even at the graves of the prophets. None of the early generations of this community or its imams did this. Rather, it is a form of associating partners with Allah. Allah Most High says:

“And they said, ‘Never abandon your gods; never abandon Wadd, Suwāʿ, Yaghūth, Yaʿūq, or Nasr.’”

“They have indeed led many astray.”

It has already been explained that these were the names of righteous men among the people of Noah, and that people remained devoted to their graves for a time. Then, after a long period had passed, they made statues of them. This is especially so when these practices are accompanied by supplicating to the deceased and seeking his aid. We have already discussed this and explained the association of partners with Allah that it involves. We have also explained the difference between “innovated visitation,” whose practitioners imitate the Christians, and “visitation prescribed by the religion.”

As for lowering one’s head before eminent figures, whether shaykhs or others, kissing the ground, and similar acts, the imams are unanimous in forbidding them. Indeed, merely bending one’s back before anyone other than Allah, Mighty and Majestic, is forbidden. It is related in al-Musnad and elsewhere:

“When Muʿādh ibn Jabal, may Allah be pleased with him, returned from Syria, he prostrated before the Prophet, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace. He said, ‘What is this, Muʿādh?’

“He replied, ‘Messenger of Allah, I saw them in Syria prostrating before their bishops and patriarchs, and they attribute this practice to their prophets.’

“He said, ‘They have lied, Muʿādh. If I were to command anyone to prostrate before another, I would command a woman to prostrate before her husband, because of the great right he has over her. Muʿādh, tell me: if you passed by my grave, would you prostrate?’

“He replied, ‘No.’

“He said, ‘Do not do this.’”

Or words to that effect spoken by the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace.

---

## Page 13177

Indeed, it is established in the Sahīh, in a hadith narrated by Jābir:

“He, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace, led his Companions in prayer while seated because of an illness. They prayed standing, so he commanded them to sit and said, ‘Do not exalt me as non-Arabs exalt one another.’”

He also said:

“Whoever is pleased to have people stand before him, let him take his seat in the Fire.”

Since he forbade them to stand while he was seated—even though they were standing in prayer—so that they would not resemble those who stand before their dignitaries, and explained that whoever is pleased to have people stand before him is among the people of the Fire, then what of prostrating before him, lowering one’s head, and kissing hands? ʿUmar ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz, may Allah be pleased with him—who was Allah’s vicegerent on earth—appointed attendants to prevent those entering his presence from kissing the ground, and would discipline those attendants if anyone kissed the ground.

In short, standing, sitting, bowing, and prostrating are the right of the One who alone is worshipped, the Creator of the heavens and the earth. Whatever is exclusively Allah’s right admits no share for anyone else. Swearing oaths is one such right, so one must not swear by anyone other than Allah, Mighty and Majestic. The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace, said:

“Whoever wishes to swear an oath, let him swear by Allah or remain silent.”

Agreed upon by al-Bukhārī and Muslim. He also said:

“Whoever swears by anyone other than Allah has associated partners with Him.”

All worship, therefore, belongs to Allah alone, with no partner:

“They were commanded only to worship Allah, sincerely devoting their religion to Him, inclining to the truth, to establish prayer, and to give zakah. That is the upright religion.”

It is also reported in the Sahīh that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace, said:

“Indeed,

---

## Page 13178

Allah is pleased with three things from you: that you worship Him without associating anything with Him; that you hold firmly, all together, to the rope of Allah and do not become divided; and that you offer sincere counsel to those whom Allah has placed in authority over you.”

Sincere devotion to Allah alone is the foundation of worship. Our Prophet, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace, forbade associating partners with Allah in all its forms, small and great, trivial and grave. Indeed, mass-transmitted reports establish that he prohibited prayer at sunrise and sunset, expressing this in various ways. Sometimes he said:

“Do not deliberately time your prayers to coincide with sunrise or sunset.”

At other times, he prohibited prayer after dawn breaks until the sun rises, and after the afternoon prayer until the sun sets. On other occasions, he explained that the sun rises between the two horns of a devil, and that the disbelievers prostrate to it at that time. He prohibited prayer at this time because it involves resembling the polytheists, who prostrate to the sun then, and because the devil positions himself alongside the sun at that moment so that the prostration will be directed to him. What, then, of practices that involve even more evident association of partners with Allah and resemblance to the polytheists?

Allah Most High said, in the words He commanded His Messenger to address to the People of the Book:

“Say, ‘O People of the Book, come to a common word between us and you: that we worship none but Allah, associate nothing with Him, and do not take one another as lords besides Allah.’ But if they turn away, then say, ‘Bear witness that we are Muslims.’”

This is because such conduct resembles that of the People of the Book in taking one another as lords besides Allah, and we are forbidden to do anything of this kind. And whoever

---

## Page 13179

departs from the guidance of his Prophet, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace, and the guidance of his Companions and those who followed them in righteousness, turning instead to ways resembling those of the Christians, has abandoned what Allah and His Messenger commanded.

As for someone saying, “My need was fulfilled through Allah’s blessing and yours,” this is a reprehensible statement. No one else is to be coupled with Allah in such expressions. Indeed, when someone said to the Prophet, may Allah bless him and his family and grant him peace, “Whatever Allah wills and you will,” he replied:

“Have you made me an equal to Allah? Rather, say: ‘Whatever Allah alone wills.’”

He also told his Companions:

“Do not say, ‘Whatever Allah wills and Muhammad wills.’ Instead, say, ‘Whatever Allah wills, then Muhammad wills.’”
