This is a lie, for Abū Barzah was not with Yazīd in Syria; he was in Iraq. As for “the body of al-Husayn,” it is in Karbalā’, by agreement.

Abū al-‘Abbās said:

Trustworthy people have told me—some reporting from Ibn Daqīq al-‘Īd, some from Abū Muhammad ‘Abd al-Mu’min ibn Khalaf al-Dimyātī, some from Abū Bakr Muhammad ibn Ahmad ibn al-Qastallānī, and some from Abū ‘Abd Allah al-Qurtubī, the Quran commentator—that each of these scholars rejected the claims concerning this shrine and said that they were false: it contained neither al-Husayn’s grave nor any part of his remains. For every one of these scholars, someone whose veracity I do not question reported this to me from him. In the case of some, a great many people reported it to me, each relating what one of these scholars had told him. Those who reported to me from Ibn al-Qastallānī related that he said, “It contains only a Christian.”

Another is “the grave of ‘Alī, may Allah be pleased with him,” within al-Najaf. What is known among the scholars is that ‘Alī was buried in the governor’s palace in Kufa, just as Mu‘āwiyah was buried in the governor’s palace in Syria, and ‘Amr was buried in the governor’s palace, out of fear that the Khārijites would dig up their graves. It has been said, however, that the grave in al-Najaf is that of al-Mughīrah

---

## Page 13578

ibn Shu‘bah. For more than three hundred years, no one claimed that it was ‘Alī’s grave, nor did anyone visit it.

Another is “the grave of ‘Abd Allah ibn ‘Umar” in al-Jazīrah. There is agreement that ‘Abd Allah ibn ‘Umar died in Mecca in the year Ibn al-Zubayr was killed. He requested that he be buried outside the Sacred Precinct because he was one of the Emigrants. This proved difficult for them, so they buried him in the upper part of Mecca.

Another is “the grave of Jābir” outside Harrān. There is agreement that Jābir died in the Prophet’s city, Medina, and that he was the last of the Companions to die there. Another is a grave in Syria attributed to “Umm Kulthūm” and “Ruqayyah.” There is agreement that both died in Medina during the Prophet’s lifetime, peace and blessings be upon him, each while married to ‘Uthmān.

This confusion arises simply from people sharing the same names. Perhaps someone bearing the name of one of those mentioned died and was buried in one of these places, and some ignorant people then assumed that the deceased was one of the Companions.

---

## Page 13579

He—may Allah have mercy on him—was asked about people living in Cairo who take their sacrificial animals and slaughter them at al-Qarāfah.

He replied:

It is not prescribed for anyone to slaughter an Eid sacrifice, or any other animal, at graves. Indeed, none of the primary acts of worship, such as prayer, fasting or charity, is prescribed at graves. Whoever thinks that offering sacrifices at graves is recommended, or that doing so is better, is ignorant and astray, opposing the consensus of the Muslims. Rather:

“The Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings be upon him, prohibited slaughtering animals at graves.”

Some people in the pre-Islamic age of ignorance used to do this: when a prominent person among them died, they would slaughter animals at his grave. The Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, forbade taking graves as places of prayer and cursed those who did so, warning his community against resembling the polytheists who venerated graves until they worshipped their occupants. How, then, can anyone make a grave a place of sacrifice, deliberately seeking to offer sacrifices there? This too is an imitation of the polytheists.

The intimate friend of Allah—may Allah’s blessings and peace be upon him—said:

“Indeed, my prayer, my sacrifice, my living and my dying are for Allah, Lord of all worlds.”

One must therefore be sincere in one’s devotion to Allah and dedicate prayer and sacrifice to Him, even if the worshipper does not intend to slaughter

---

## Page 13580

at a grave; nevertheless, Islamic law has blocked the means leading to wrongdoing, just as the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, prohibited prayer at sunrise and sunset because the disbelievers prostrate to the sun at those times, even though someone praying to Allah does not intend to do that.

Likewise, he prohibited taking graves as places of prayer, even when the worshipper prays only to Allah. He said:

“Whoever imitates those other than us is not one of us.”

He also said:

“Whoever imitates a people is one of them.”

And Allah knows best.

He was asked about a man who went one morning to “al-Takrūrī” to look on and drowned: “Is he a sinner or a martyr?”

He replied:

Those who set out for this grave to pray beside it, use it as a means in supplication, rub themselves against it, kiss it, or do similar prohibited things; or to engage in anything Allah has forbidden, such as indecent acts, drinking wine, or playing pipes; or to watch these people and observe those committing sins without denouncing them—are disobeying Allah by making this journey. Their affair rests with Allah Most High, and it is hoped that, through their drowning, they will receive Allah’s mercy. And Allah knows best.

---

## Page 13581

He was asked—may Allah have mercy on him:

“Are there righteous people in this community whom Allah has hidden from others, so that no one sees them except those whom they choose? Even if they are among people, are they concealed by virtue of their spiritual state? Are there forty men on Mount Lebanon hidden from view, who, whenever one of them dies, take another person to replace him, who then becomes hidden along with them just as they are? Are distances shortened for all these people, so that they perform Hajj and cover in an hour journeys that would take a month or a year? Do some of them fly like birds, speak of unseen events before they occur, eat bones and clay and find them palatable and sweet, and do other such things?”

He replied:

All praise is due to Allah, Lord of the worlds. As for the existence of people who remain perpetually concealed from others, this is false. No prophet, friend of Allah, or sorcerer has ever possessed such a power.

However, a person may sometimes be concealed from certain people, either as a miraculous gift bestowed upon a friend of Allah or through sorcery. Some of these states come from the Most Merciful: these are the miraculous gifts bestowed upon the friends of Allah who follow the Quran and Sunnah—the believers who are mindful of Allah. Other states arise from the soul or from Satan, as happens to some

---

## Page 13582

disbelievers, to whom hidden matters are sometimes disclosed, and to some soothsayers, whom devils inform about certain things. The states experienced by the people of religious innovation belong to this category.

Some of these people are carried by devils, who fly with them through the air. Some dance in the air. Others are possessed by a devil, so that they feel neither blows nor fire when thrown into it. Yet the fire does not become cool or safe for them, for that happens only to those whose states come from the Most Merciful. Those who perform “signs”—corrupt practices involving lādhan, saffron, rosewater, and other substances—belong to this group. Most of them practise fraudulent deception, while their elite experience satanic states. There is no friend of Allah among them; rather, they are brethren of the devils, of the same kind as the Tatars.

There are no forty men residing on Mount Lebanon or elsewhere in this manner, nor are there people who remain perpetually hidden from human sight. The hadith stating that the abdāl (replacements) number forty men is weak. Allah’s friends who are mindful of Him increase or decrease in number according to the prevalence or scarcity of faith and mindfulness of Allah. At the beginning of Islam, they numbered fewer than forty; when Islam spread, they became more numerous than that.

As for traversing great distances, this happens to some righteous people and also to some brethren of the devils. It is not among the greatest miraculous gifts; rather, the one who

---

## Page 13583

performs Hajj with the Muslims is greater than one who makes the pilgrimage through the air.

Thus, Shaykh Ibrāhīm al-Jaʿbarī met some people who used to make the pilgrimage through the air. They asked him to accompany them, but he said, “This Hajj does not discharge your obligation until you perform Hajj as the Muslims do, and as the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and his Companions did.” They agreed to this, and after completing Hajj they said, “We have never performed a more blessed pilgrimage than this one: in it we tasted the sweetness of worshipping Allah and obeying Him.” This happens only at certain times; it is not available to a person whenever he seeks it.

Likewise, hidden matters are sometimes disclosed to Allah’s friends and sometimes to the brethren of the devils. Those whose states are satanic may consume foul foods, even eating excrement and other filthy things under the influence of a satanic state. They are blameworthy for this. For Allah’s friends are those who follow the Messenger, the unlettered Prophet, who commands them to do what is right, forbids them from what is wrong, permits them wholesome things, and forbids them foul things. Whoever consumes foul things has satanic states, for these states are the fruits of one’s deeds.

Eating wholesome things and doing righteous deeds give rise to states that come from the Most Merciful, including disclosures of hidden matters and effects that Allah and His Messenger love. Eating foul things and committing wrongful deeds give rise to satanic states that Allah and His Messenger hate. The protectors of the Tatars are among these people.

---

## Page 13584

And when they come together with someone whose spiritual state comes from the Most Merciful, their own states lose their effect and their devils flee. They gain prominence only among unbelievers and the ignorant, just as performers of extraordinary feats gain prominence among the Tatars, the Bedouins, peasants, and other ignorant people who know neither the Book nor the Sunnah. But when the followers of Muhammad, who adhere to the Book and the Sunnah, are present, these people’s states lose their effect. And Allah knows best.

What do the leading scholars of religion say about the Prophet’s worship, may Allah bless him and grant him peace: what was its nature, and how did he worship before his mission? Give us your ruling; may you be rewarded.

He answered:

Praise be to Allah. This is a question we have no need to resolve under our revealed law. Our duty is simply to obey the Messenger in what he has commanded us and to follow his example after he was sent to us.

As for what preceded that, such as his worship in the cave of Hirā and similar practices, these are not prescribed practices for the Muslim community to follow. Thus, after embracing Islam, none of the Companions would go to the cave of Hirā or deliberately seek out anything similar. Since the advent of Islam, it has not been prescribed for us to seek out mountain caves or withdraw into seclusion there. Rather, the prescribed practice for us is to observe devotional retreats in mosques. As for deliberately seeking seclusion in mountain caves and caverns and travelling to a mountain

---

## Page 13585

to seek blessings—such as Mount Sinai, Mount Hirā, the mountain of Yathrib, or the like—this is not prescribed for us. Indeed, he, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“No journey should be undertaken except to three mosques.”

Before his mission, he, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, used to perform Hajj, give charity, shoulder others’ burdens, honour guests, and help others face hardships in worthy causes. He did not follow the religion of his polytheistic people. May Allah bestow abundant blessings and peace upon him and his Companions.

---

## Page 13586

He also said:

Section

As for deliberately choosing to pray, supplicate, and worship in a place where the prophets did not deliberately choose to pray and worship, but where they are merely reported to have passed through, stopped, or lived—as explained above, neither Ibn ʿUmar nor anyone else used to do this. It does not constitute following them, either in an action they performed or in an intention they had.

It is well known that the places where the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, stayed—whether travelling or residing somewhere—such as the routes he took during his Hajj and military expeditions, his stopping places on his journeys, the houses in which he lived, and the houses he would sometimes visit, from among … (1)

“So do not take graves as places of prayer, for I forbid you to do so.”

These explicit statements of his establish that taking their graves as places of prayer is forbidden, even though they are buried there, are alive in their graves, and visiting their graves to greet them with peace is recommended. Nevertheless, visiting them to pray beside them and taking them as places of prayer is forbidden. It is well known that he prohibited this only because it leads to associating partners with Allah, and he intended that

> **Footnotes:**
> (1) Omitted from the original.

---

## Page 13587

mosques should be devoted exclusively to Allah Most High, built solely for His worship, with no created being given a share in that worship. If building a mosque on account of a deceased person is forbidden, the same applies when it is built on account of some other relic, for associating partners with Allah occurs in both cases. This is why Christians used to build churches over the graves of prophets and righteous men, at sites associated with them, and in their names.

This is what Umar, may Allah be pleased with him, feared the Muslims would fall into, and what the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, intended to prevent his community from doing. As Allah Most High says:

“The places of worship belong to Allah, so do not invoke anyone alongside Allah.”

And He Most High says:

“Say: My Lord has commanded justice. Devote yourselves to Him at every place of worship, and call upon Him with sincere devotion to Him alone.”

And He Most High says:

“It is not for those who associate partners with Allah to maintain Allah’s mosques while bearing witness against themselves to their disbelief. Their deeds have come to nothing, and they will remain in the Fire forever.”

“Only those who believe in Allah and the Last Day, establish prayer, give zakah, and fear none but Allah maintain Allah’s mosques. It is they who may hope to be among the rightly guided.”

Had this been recommended, it would have been recommended for the Companions and their successors to pray in all the chambers of his wives and in every place where he stayed during his military expeditions or journeys. It would also have been recommended for them to build mosques there. Yet the early generations did none of this. Allah Most High has not prescribed for Muslims any place to seek out specifically for prayer except mosques, nor any place to seek out specifically for worship except the designated ritual sites. Thus, the pilgrimage sites, such as Arafah, Muzdalifah, and Minā,

---

## Page 13588

are sought out for remembrance of Allah, supplication, and proclaiming His greatness, not for prayer, unlike mosques, which are sought out for prayer. No particular place is to be singled out for worship except mosques and the designated ritual sites, where prayer and ritual worship are performed. Allah Most High says:

“Say: Indeed, my prayer, my sacrifice, my living and my dying are for Allah, Lord of the worlds. He has no partner. This is what I have been commanded.”

As for other places, it is not recommended to seek out any particular spot for prayer, supplication, or remembrance of Allah, since neither Allah nor His Messenger has prescribed seeking it out for these purposes—even if it was a prophet’s dwelling, a place where he stayed, or somewhere he passed through.

The foundation of religion is to follow the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and conform to his guidance by doing what he commanded and prescribed for us and established as our practice. We emulate him in those actions in which he prescribed that we follow his example, unlike those matters that were exclusive to him. As for an action that he neither prescribed nor commanded for us, nor performed in a manner that established it as an example for us to follow, it is not an act of worship or a means of drawing nearer to Allah. Treating it as such therefore goes against his guidance, may Allah bless him and grant him peace.

Whatever permissible acts he performed without intending worship, we too may perform as permissible acts, just as he did. But is it prescribed for us to make them acts of worship and means of drawing nearer to Allah? There are two views on this, as explained earlier. Most of the early generations and scholars held that we should not make them acts of worship or means of drawing nearer to Allah. Rather, we should follow his example in the manner in which he performed them: if he did something merely as a permissible act, we do it as a permissible act; and if he did it to draw nearer to Allah, we do it to draw nearer to Allah.

Those who treated such an act as worship considered this a fuller way of following his example and resembling him. They also believed that it contained blessing because of its particular association with him.

---

## Page 13589

He also said—may Allah have mercy on him:

Section

The virtues of al-Shām (Greater Syria) and its people are established by the Quran, the Sunnah, and reports transmitted from scholars. These virtues were among the grounds on which I relied when urging the Muslims to fight the Tatars, instructing them to remain in Damascus, forbidding them to flee to Egypt, calling upon the Egyptian army to come to al-Shām, and encouraging the Syrians to stand firm there. Many events unfolded in this connection.

These virtues include the following:

First: the blessing bestowed upon this land. This is established by five verses from the Book of Allah Most High. In the account of Moses, Allah Most High says:

“They said, ‘We were harmed before you came to us and after you came to us.’ He said, ‘Perhaps your Lord will destroy your enemy.’”

—and continues to His words:

“But when We lifted the punishment from them until their appointed time, they immediately broke their word.”

“So We took vengeance upon them and drowned them in the sea, because they denied Our signs and were heedless of them.”

“And We caused the people who had been oppressed to inherit the eastern and western parts of the land which We had blessed. And the good promise of your Lord to the Children of Israel was fulfilled because of their patience.”

It is well known that

---

## Page 13590

the Children of Israel were given as their inheritance only the eastern and western parts of al-Shām, after Pharaoh had been drowned in the sea. Allah Most High also says:

“Glory be to Him who took His servant by night from the Sacred Mosque to al-Aqsā Mosque, whose surroundings We have blessed.”

Its surroundings are the land of al-Shām. Allah Most High says in the account of Abraham:

“They plotted against him, but We made them the greatest losers.”

“And We delivered him and Lot to the land which We had blessed for all peoples.”

It is well known that Allah delivered Abraham and Lot from the lands of al-Jazīra and the Euphrates to the land of al-Shām. Allah Most High also says:

“And to Solomon We subjected the wind, blowing forcefully, flowing at his command towards the land which We had blessed.”

It flowed only towards the land of al-Shām, where Solomon’s kingdom lay.

Allah Most High says in the account of Sheba:

“And We placed between them and the towns which We had blessed other towns within sight of one another, and set measured stages of travel between them.”

Both belonged to the ancient settlements between Yemen—the dwelling places of Sheba—and the farthest reaches of al-Shām, as scholars have mentioned.

These, then, are five passages in which Allah mentions the land of al-Shām in connection with Abraham’s migration to it, the Messenger’s night journey to it, the Children of Israel’s relocation to it, Solomon’s kingdom there, and the journeys of the people of Sheba to it. He describes it as “the land which We had blessed.”

It also contains the Mount on which Allah spoke to Moses, and by which Allah swore in Surah al-Tūr (The Mount), and in:

“By the fig and the olive,”

“and by Mount Sinai.”

And in it is

---

## Page 13591

al-Aqsā Mosque. It is where the prophets of the Children of Israel were sent, where Abraham migrated, and where our Prophet was taken on his night journey and from where he ascended. There lie his dominion, the mainstay of his religion and his Book, and a victorious group from his nation. To it will be the gathering and the return, just as the beginning is in Mecca.

Mecca is the Mother of Towns, from beneath which the earth was spread out, while al-Shām is where people will be gathered. In His words:

“At the first gathering,”

He drew attention to the second gathering. Thus Mecca is the starting point and Īliyā (Jerusalem) the place of return in creation, and likewise in divine command, for the Messenger was taken by night from Mecca to Īliyā.

His prophetic mission and his religion originated in Mecca, while the perfection, ascendancy and completion of his religion, extending to the kingdom of the Mahdī, are in al-Shām. Thus Mecca is the beginning and al-Shām the end: in creation and command, in the divine words concerning the created order and religion.

Among these merits is that a victorious group will remain there until the Hour comes. It is this group concerning which the hadith is established in the authentic collections, through Muʿāwiya and others:

“A group of my nation will remain triumphant upon the truth, unharmed by those who oppose them or abandon them, until the Hour comes.”

In the two Sahīh collections, Muʿādh ibn Jabal says:

“They are in al-Shām.”

In al-Bukhārī’s Tārīkh (History), it is reported as a statement of the Prophet:

“They are in Damascus.”

In Sahīh Muslim, the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“The people of the West will remain triumphant, unharmed by those who oppose them, until the Hour comes.”

Ahmad ibn Hanbal said: “The people of the West are the people of al-Shām.” They are indeed as he said, for two reasons. First: the other hadith reports make clear that they are the people of al-Shām.

---

## Page 13592

Second: in the usage of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and the people of his city, “the people of the West” means the people of al-Shām and those farther west, just as “the people of the East” means the people of Najd and Iraq. East and west are relative: every place has a west that may lie east of another place, and an east that may lie west of another. Thus, the Prophet’s words must be understood relative to what lay west and east of him where he uttered this hadith, namely Medina.

Anyone familiar with geographical calculations, such as longitude and latitude, knows that Harrān, al-Raqqa and Sīmsiyāt lie along Mecca’s meridian, and that the Euphrates and the lands on either bank—or rather, most of this area—lie along Medina’s meridian. The two cities differ by two degrees of longitude. Whatever lies west of the Euphrates therefore lies west of Medina, and whatever lies east of it lies east of Medina.

He thus foretold that the people of the West would remain triumphant, whereas the people of the East might prevail at one time and be defeated at another. This is indeed what has happened: the army of al-Shām has continued to enjoy victory. The people of Medina used to call al-Awzāʿī the imam of the people of the West, while describing al-Thawrī as an easterner and one of the people of the East.

Among its merits is that it is Allah’s chosen land, or that its people are Allah’s chosen people and the best people on earth. Abū Dāwūd cited two hadiths in his Sunan as evidence for this:

The report of ʿAbd Allāh ibn Khawāla al-Azdī, from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, who said:

“You will organize yourselves

---

## Page 13593

into armies: an army in al-Shām, an army in Yemen, and an army in Iraq. Al-Khawālī said, ‘O Messenger of Allah, choose for me.’ He replied, ‘Go to al-Shām, for it is Allah’s chosen land, to which He draws His chosen servants. Whoever refuses should go to Yemen and beware of its treachery, for Allah has pledged to me to care for al-Shām and its people.’”

Al-Khawālī used to say: “Whoever is under Allah’s care will not be forsaken.” This hadith thus mentions its merits, including that it is Allah’s chosen land.

There is also the hadith of ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr, who reported that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“There will be a migration after a migration. The best people on earth will be those who remain most firmly in the land to which Abraham migrated. The worst people will remain elsewhere on earth: their lands will cast them out, and the Most Merciful Himself will loathe them. The fire will gather them together with apes and swine, spending the night with them wherever they spend the night, and resting at midday with them wherever they rest at midday.”

He thus stated that the best people on earth are those who remain most firmly in the land to which Abraham migrated, unlike those who come to it or leave it. The land to which Abraham migrated is al-Shām.

This hadith brings glad tidings to our companions who migrated from Harrān and elsewhere to the land of Abraham’s migration and followed Abraham’s faith and the religion of their Prophet Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him abundant peace. It also explains that theirs is a migration after the migration of the Companions of Allah’s Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, to Medina. For migration is to the place of the Messenger and the enduring traces of his legacy. For us, the land of Abraham’s migration has been made equivalent to the land of our Prophet’s migration, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, since migration to the latter came to an end with the conquest of Mecca.

Another of its merits is the Prophet’s instruction to go there, in the hadith recorded by al-Tirmidhī.

---

## Page 13594

Another of its merits is that Allah has pledged to care for al-Shām and its people, as stated in the hadith of al-Khawālī. Another is that:

“The angels of the Most Merciful spread their wings over al-Shām,”

as reported in the authentic hadith narrated by Abd Allāh ibn Umar. Another is that the pillar of the Book and of Islam is in al-Shām, as the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“I saw what appeared to be the pillar of the Book being taken from beneath my head. I followed it with my eyes, and it was carried to al-Shām.”

Another is that it is the heartland of the believers, as the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“And the heartland of the believers is al-Shām.”

Another is that its hypocrites will not prevail over its believers, as Ahmad related in a hadith in al-Musnad. I cited this as evidence to some chief judges and others during times of turmoil, when people given to wickedness and religious innovation, bearing the traits of hypocrites, rose against us. When they tried to frighten us with these people, I told them of this hadith and that our hypocrites would not prevail over our believers.

These prophetic statements were fulfilled in the fullest measure in our jihad against the Tatars. Allah showed the Muslims the truth of what we had promised them and the blessings of what we had instructed them to do. It was a tremendous victory, the like of which the Muslims had not witnessed since the rise of the Tatar empire that had humiliated the people of Islam. For never had they been defeated and overcome as they were overcome

---

## Page 13595

at the “Gate of Damascus” in the great campaign, during which Allah bestowed upon us blessings beyond counting, both particular and general.

All praise is due to Allah, Lord of all worlds—abundant, good and blessed praise, such as our Lord loves and is pleased with, and as befits the generosity of His Face and the might of His majesty.
